`CMakeLists.txt`:

```txt
cmake_minimum_required(VERSION 3.20)
cmake_policy(SET CMP0091 NEW)
project(fc6hack LANGUAGES CXX)

set(CMAKE_CXX_STANDARD 20)
set(CMAKE_CXX_STANDARD_REQUIRED ON)

if(NOT CMAKE_SIZEOF_VOID_P EQUAL 8)
    message(FATAL_ERROR "Far Cry 6 is 64-bit: configure with an x64 toolchain (-A x64).")
endif()

if(MSVC)
    add_compile_options(/W4 /permissive- /utf-8)
    add_compile_definitions(WIN32_LEAN_AND_MEAN NOMINMAX UNICODE _UNICODE _CRT_SECURE_NO_WARNINGS)
endif()

# Shared code: process access, pattern scanning, math.
add_library(fc6common STATIC
    src/common/process.cpp
    src/common/pattern.cpp
    src/common/heap.cpp
)
target_include_directories(fc6common PUBLIC src)

# External probe: attaches to the running game and reads memory.
add_executable(fc6probe src/probe/main.cpp src/probe/matrices.cpp src/probe/ptrscan.cpp src/probe/near.cpp src/probe/values.cpp src/probe/world.cpp)
target_link_libraries(fc6probe PRIVATE fc6common)

# Internal DLL (loaded into the game, offline only): Havok ray casts served over shared memory.
add_library(fc6internal SHARED src/internal/dllmain.cpp src/internal/aim.cpp src/internal/game.cpp
    src/internal/skeleton.cpp src/internal/targeting.cpp src/internal/profiles.cpp src/internal/config.cpp
    src/internal/hud.cpp src/internal/overlay.cpp src/internal/painter.cpp src/internal/esp.cpp src/internal/menu.cpp
    src/internal/canvas.cpp src/internal/hud_view.cpp src/internal/gamethread.cpp src/internal/inventory.cpp)
target_link_libraries(fc6internal PRIVATE d2d1 dwrite d3d11 dxgi dcomp)
target_include_directories(fc6internal PRIVATE src)
set_property(TARGET fc6internal PROPERTY MSVC_RUNTIME_LIBRARY "MultiThreaded")

# Overlay preview: the menu, HUD and cone drawn into a PNG with sample data, without the game.
add_executable(fc6preview src/preview/main.cpp src/internal/hud_view.cpp src/internal/menu.cpp
    src/internal/painter.cpp src/internal/config.cpp src/internal/game.cpp src/internal/hud.cpp
    src/internal/inventory.cpp src/internal/gamethread.cpp)
target_link_libraries(fc6preview PRIVATE d2d1 dwrite windowscodecs ole32)
target_include_directories(fc6preview PRIVATE src)

# Injector for the internal DLL.
add_executable(fc6inject src/injector/main.cpp)
target_link_libraries(fc6inject PRIVATE fc6common)

```

`README.md`:

```md
# farcry-6-hack

Reverse engineering **Far Cry 6**: a C++ DLL loaded into the game that reads its memory and reuses
the game's own systems (the aim-assist target list, animated skeletons, Havok collision queries,
item functions). It provides an aimbot, a skeleton ESP, a HUD and an in-game menu. A learning
project, for **offline single-player only**.

> ⚠️ **Single-player, offline (Ubisoft Connect in offline mode).** Never in co-op or online: your
> Ubisoft account could be banned. Nothing is changed on disk; everything happens in memory, for
> the length of the session. The game ships no anti-cheat, but that is no reason to use this
> against other players.

## Features

**Aimbot** (hold a key, right mouse button by default)
- **Targets**: the game's aim-assist target list, filtered to hostile humans (attitude read live);
  never allies, civilians, animals or the dead. A "threats first" option puts NPCs whose AI is
  aiming at the player first.
- **Aim point**: each character's animated skeleton (428 to 458 bones, found by the CRC32 of their
  names): head, then neck, chest and pelvis. If the weapon's spread would miss the head half of the
  time at that range, it aims at the chest.
- **Line of sight**: rays cast with the game's own collision query and its bullet filter, from the
  game's fire origin. An enemy with no exposed part is skipped.
- **Exact ballistics**: speed and gravity read from the held weapon's data (attachments and loaded
  ammo included), leading moving targets and compensating bullet drop.
- **Aim with the barrel**: compensates weapon sway using the game's fire direction (useful in
  scopes).
- **View control**: writes directly to the player's view-angle accumulator (one correction per game
  frame, recoil left to the game), or, optionally, calibrated simulated mouse movement
  (`SendInput`).

**Overlay** (Direct2D on a DirectComposition swap chain, borderless windowed)
- **ESP**: skeleton and label of living NPCs, coloured by the aimbot's decision, with "TE VISE"
  ("aiming at you") above those targeting the player.
- **HUD**: status, weapon, target and events; aim cone circle.
- **In-game menu** (Insert, French UI): Settings and Inventory tabs, switches and sliders, settings
  saved to `fc6hack.ini`.
- **Inventory**: counts of stackable items (money, materials…) read and changed by the game's own
  functions, called on the game's thread.

**Reverse engineering tools**: `fc6probe.exe` (value, pointer and matrix scans) and ~35 Python
scripts (standard library only) to read memory from outside, watch what writes to an address
(debug registers, no code patch), record bullet trajectories, measure spread, find fields by their
behaviour…

## Requirements

- Windows 10/11 x64.
- Far Cry 6, Steam version, **build 11359732**. Offsets depend on this version: a game update
  breaks them.
- Visual Studio 2022 (MSVC x64) with CMake.
- Python 3.10 or later, for the tools only.

## Build

```bash
cmake -S . -B build -G "Visual Studio 17 2022" -A x64
cmake --build build --config Release
```

Produces in `build/Release/`: `fc6internal.dll` (the DLL), `fc6inject.exe` (the injector),
`fc6probe.exe` (exploration tool) and `fc6preview.exe` (renders the menu and HUD to a PNG, without
the game).

## Usage

1. Start Far Cry 6 **offline**, in **borderless windowed** mode, and load a save.
2. Load the DLL (again after every game launch):
   ```bash
   build/Release/fc6inject.exe
   ```
3. In game:

   | Key | Action |
   |---|---|
   | Insert | open / close the menu |
   | ↑ ↓ ← → | navigate and adjust in the menu |
   | Page Up / Page Down | switch tabs (Settings / Inventory) |
   | F6 | aimbot on/off |
   | F7 | ESP on/off |
   | right mouse button (configurable) | hold to aim |

Settings and learned values are in `build/Release/fc6hack.ini`, the log in `fc6hack.log`, next to
the DLL. To unload the DLL: `python tools/rays.py --unload`.

Simulated-mouse mode needs a calibration, to redo after changing the in-game sensitivity:
`python tools/calibrate.py`.

## Layout

```
src/
  internal/   fc6internal.dll: game reads, aimbot, ESP, HUD, menu, inventory
  injector/   fc6inject.exe: loads the DLL into the game (remote LoadLibraryW)
  probe/      fc6probe.exe: command-line exploration
  preview/    fc6preview.exe: menu and HUD preview as a PNG
  common/     process access, signatures, maths, heap walking
tools/        Python scripts: prototypes (aimbot, ESP) and research tools
docs/         reversing.md: structures, offsets and the method that found them
offsets/      pointer chains to the camera
```

## How it works

Everything is documented in [`docs/reversing.md`](docs/reversing.md): each structure, each offset,
how it was found and how it was checked in game. Some key points:

- **No code patching.** To intercept a game function (the ray query, the view loop), the DLL sets
  hardware breakpoints (DR0–DR3) handled by a vectored exception handler.
- **The game's collision queries, replayed.** The DLL copies a real ray query from the game, then
  replays it from its own thread with its own Havok allocator (`hkLifoAllocator`).
- **Game functions on the game's thread.** The inventory calls the game's item functions from the
  game's own thread: called from the overlay thread, they crashed the game.
- **Measured cost.** Memory reads guarded by SEH with a cache of `VirtualQuery` regions, culling
  before any skeleton read, and a capped frame rate: the ESP's gather pass went from about 4 ms to
  0.1 ms per frame. The lessons are summed up in
  [`docs/kb/in-process-overlay-lessons.md`](docs/kb/in-process-overlay-lessons.md).

## Limitations

- Offsets only hold for build 11359732; no signatures yet.
- A distant character whose skeleton the game no longer animates has a frozen pose: the head is
  then estimated.
- The in-game menu is in French.
- Tested on a single machine (Windows 11, Steam).

## Credits

- Tools: Ghidra, Cheat Engine and a lot of in-game testing.
- Far Cry 6 belongs to Ubisoft. This project is not affiliated with or endorsed by Ubisoft and
  contains no game files.

## License

[MIT](LICENSE).

```

`docs/kb/in-process-overlay-lessons.md`:

```md
---
kind: technique
title: 'In-process overlay and view control for a single-player game: cost and stability lessons'
status: working
agents: []
humans: []
date: '2026-10-06'
links: []
tags: [native-hook, dll, overlay, direct2d, memory-reading, virtualquery, camera, view-control, havok, raycast, line-of-sight, performance, oracle, far-cry-6]
---
# In-process overlay and view control for a single-player game: cost and stability lessons

> Lessons from an injected DLL that reads a native game's state, draws an overlay (labels and skeletons
> over characters) and turns the player's view, in Far Cry 6 offline. They cover what made it slow, why the
> view shook or bounced, and why the game's own ray query disagreed with its bullets. Every number below was
> measured in the running game, mostly with traces the DLL wrote and scripts replayed. All of it applies to
> any native game read from inside the process.

## When to use it
- You read game memory from your own threads (an overlay, a helper, a camera tool) and the frame time or
  your own loop is worse than it should be.
- You write the game's view angles, or move the mouse, from a loop of your own. The view overshoots,
  trembles, or fights the game after a shot.
- You use the game's physics ray query for "can X be seen/hit from here" and it disagrees with what the
  game's bullets actually do.
- Scope: offline single-player only. Don't use any of this in multiplayer or against anti-cheat.

## How

**Measure before optimizing.** Time each pass with the wall clock and the thread's own CPU time
(`QueryThreadCycleTime`, cycles divided by an `rdtsc` rate measured once). Then split the slow pass into
steps and log the per-step averages every 10 s.
- Wall time much larger than CPU time means waiting (GPU, display, no free core).
- Equal times mean real work.

Here, two plausible guesses were wrong: text layout, then waiting on the GPU. The per-step timer pointed at a
single function within one round trip.

**Memory reads that can't crash, at no cost.**
- Guard each copy with SEH (`__try`, access violations only).
- Validate pointers with `VirtualQuery` before reading, but cache the *whole region* it returns
  (`BaseAddress`, `RegionSize`) instead of the page:
  - the cache is per thread (a `std::map` keyed by start address, disjoint ranges), kept ~20 s;
  - an entry is dropped when a guarded copy faults anyway;
  - writes use the same cache.

**Per-entity lookups:** cache "entity → component pointer", and revalidate it with 3 reads (array pointer
unchanged, slot still holds it, vtable still matches) instead of rescanning the entity's component slots.

**Overlay:**
- Cull before reading: project the cheap root position first, and skip characters that are behind the
  camera or off screen before touching skeletons, health or AI.
- Keep text layouts between frames (DirectWrite `CreateTextLayout` per label per frame), evicting those
  unused for ~30 frames.
- Cap the overlay's frame rate. A flip-model swap chain with `Present(1)` otherwise redraws at the monitor's
  rate (144 Hz+), and reads everything each time.

**View control that doesn't oscillate:**
- **Correct once per new camera picture.** The camera matrix you read shows a write to the view angles only
  at the game's next frame. Measure it: write ±1° into the angle accumulator and time the matrix change (here
  8 ms, one picture, at 91 fps). A loop faster than the game corrects the same error several times.
  - Gate on "the camera matrix changed since the last correction", with a ~50 ms fallback.
- **Smooth noisy offsets on the right time scale.** The weapon's fire direction against the camera (sway
  compensation) is a slow signal, but read each tick it jumped 0.07–0.15°: the two values aren't sampled at
  the same instant. Averaged over 0.2 s, the shaking in scopes went away.
- **Leave recoil to the game.**
  - A shot shows as the camera's pitch jumping 1–6° in one picture *relative to the view accumulator*. The
    game then brings it back by itself in ~0.3 s.
  - Measure pitch from the accumulator plus a slowly followed offset, and freeze that offset from the jump
    until the camera is back (0.4 s at most).
  - Fighting the kick made the view bounce 3° past the target.
  - Detect the kick as a jump, not as a deviation: in some stances the camera−accumulator pitch offset sits
    at −11° and moves 0.6° per picture.
- **Record a trace and replay fixes offline.** Write one CSV row per tick (camera, accumulator, error,
  correction, offsets, "new picture"), flushed when the aim key is released. Each fix above was found from
  this trace and replayed on it before rebuilding: e.g. the jump detector fired once per shot and never in
  sessions without shots.

**Line of sight with the game's own ray query, checked against the game itself:**
- Cast from where the game's bullets start, not from the camera. Ask the game for its fire origin. In first
  person it equals the eye; with a third-person or vehicle camera, the camera sits behind the player's own
  vehicle, which the player's bullets go through.
- A ray stopping near the target is often the target itself (its body, its weapon) or its vehicle (glass
  that bullets go through). Accept hits close to its axis, wider when it's seated.
- **Oracle: the game's damage.** Log every health drop of an NPC with the last line-of-sight verdict for it,
  and where its last blocked ray had stopped.
  - "Lost 144 hp while 'hidden'" proves the blocker was something bullets pass through.
  - Six kills confirmed the "own body" rule.
  - This replaced manual labelling sessions, which the human, busy playing, never did.

## Gotchas
1. **Symptom:** the ESP cost 3.5–4.7 ms of CPU per frame for 1–4 characters drawn, with spikes of 30–200 ms.
   **Cause:** `VirtualQuery` computes `RegionSize` by walking the page table from the address to the end of
   the run of pages with the same attributes. The game's heaps are 200–430 MB regions, so one call took
   **up to 4.5 ms** (timed from outside with `VirtualQueryEx`: 27 ms for 30 addresses). The old cache kept
   *pages* for 1 s, so every second each touched page paid again. **Fix:** cache whole regions (one call
   covers the rest of the region); gather time fell to **0.06–0.12 ms**, worst case 0.3 ms.
2. **Symptom:** irregular aim ticks. **Cause:** the same check ran uncached before every *write* of the view
   angles, two per correction, up to ~4 ms each. **Fix:** the same region cache, with a "writable" flag.
3. **Symptom:** the overlay ran at 32 fps with 10–12 ms per frame with the ESP on, 60 fps / 0.1 ms with it
   off. **Cause:** per character and per frame, ~8 component lookups (each a scan of 96 slots and their
   vtables), done for every character within 300 m, including those behind the player; plus 2 text layouts
   rebuilt per label. **Fix:** culling, component cache and layout cache. That halved it; gotcha 1 was the rest.
4. **Symptom:** the view overshot and oscillated, worse when the frame rate dropped. **Cause:** 120 Hz
   corrections against a camera that updates once per game frame, one frame late: 2–4 corrections from the
   same picture. **Fix:** one correction per new picture.
5. **Symptom:** a ±0.03° tremble on the target, visible in high-zoom scopes. **Cause:** chasing tick-to-tick
   noise in the sway compensation term. **Fix:** a 0.2 s average; also a dead zone of 30 % of the head's
   angular radius.
6. **Symptom:** after each pistol shot the view jumped up, slammed down 3° past the target, then crept back.
   **Cause:** the aim corrected a transient camera offset that the game removes itself. **Fix:** detect the
   jump in camera−accumulator pitch and hold the offset (above).
7. **Symptom:** a "deviation > 0.35° = kick" detector stayed latched most of the time in one session.
   **Cause:** a large and moving camera−accumulator offset (−11 °, ±1°) in some stances. **Fix:** detect
   upward *jumps* above 1° per picture that the previous correction doesn't explain.
8. **Symptom:** enemies inside cars, and anything seen from the driver's seat, were "hidden" yet hittable.
   **Cause:** rays from a third-person camera through the player's own car, and rays stopping on the
   occupants' windows 0.7–1.1 m before their heads. **Fix:** rays from the game's fire origin; accept hits
   within 1.6 m of seated targets. (The fire-origin fix is deployed, but not yet confirmed in a vehicle.)
9. **Symptom:** the hit record's "material" and "object" fields changed meaning from one call to the next.
   **Cause:** the 0x60-byte output slot holds the fraction at +0x00..+0x10. The rest is unwritten stack:
   MXCSR/x87 defaults `0x1f80`/`0x27f`, ASCII from the DLL's own log formatting, one pointer shared by
   different targets. **Fix:** don't decode a raw record before checking that its fields stay consistent
   across known inputs.
10. **Symptom:** bash heredocs turned `\n` inside C++ string literals into real newlines in generated Python
    edit scripts. **Fix:** write such scripts with a file-writing tool, not a heredoc.
11. **Symptom:** a C2513 syntax error on `const float near = ...`. **Cause:** `<windows.h>` defines `near`
    and `far` as macros. **Fix:** another name.
12. **Symptom:** LNK1104 when relinking the DLL. **Cause:** it's still loaded in the game. **Fix:** ask the
    DLL to unload itself (shared-memory flag), wait, link, then inject again.
13. **Symptom:** a camera-lag measurement saw no camera change at all, and a "frame time" of 2.7 ms.
    **Cause:** the game had focus but was paused (in a menu). **Fix:** measurement scripts wait until the camera moves before
    sampling.

## Seen in
- Far Cry 6 (Steam build 11359732, Windows 11), offline: an injected DLL with a Direct2D/DirectComposition
  overlay, written to the game's view-angle accumulator. The offsets and structures stay in the project's
  own notes.

```

`docs/reversing.md`:

```md
# Far Cry 6 reversing notes

Game: Steam build 11359732. Engine module `FC_m64d3d12.dll` (all offsets below are relative to it).
Everything was found with `fc6probe` (read-only) while playing offline.

## World conventions (verified 2026-10-05)
- Z-up world, units in metres. Example map position: (1230, 3382, 314.6).
- Transform matrices are 4x4 row-major floats (`R44`):
  `row0 = right, row1 = forward, row2 = up, row3 = position (w = 1)`.
  Forward was confirmed by walking 8.5 m straight: walk direction == row1.
- Some components store the same basis as 3x4 with the translation in column 3 (`R34`), and a
  view-style variant exists with rows `right, up, -forward`.
- The player's eyes are 1.59 m above the feet.

## Camera
Pointer chains, CE style: `p = [module+static]; p = [p + o1]; address = p + o2`. The address holds
the live camera matrix (R44 above).

Kept in `offsets/camera_chains.txt`. These 5 were found in one game session and **still resolved
to the camera after a full game restart** (2026-10-05), all giving the same matrix at the same
instant:

| static | o1 | o2 |
|---|---|---|
| `0x6a66ea0` | `0x2f8` | `0x260` |
| `0x6a678e8` | `0x348` | `0x260` |
| `0x6b1b1b0` | `0x38`  | `0x350` |
| `0x6a66e80` | `0x30`  | `0x1f8` |
| `0x6b1b1e8` | `0x430` | `0x1f8` |

`fc6probe chains offsets/camera_chains.txt` reads them all at once.

Projection (2026-10-06), relative to the camera matrix address `M` of any chain above:
`M + 0x40` = x scale `1/tan(hfov/2)` (1.3032 at FOV 75), `M + 0x54` = y scale (2.3168 =
x scale × aspect), `M + 0x60..0x7f` = depth terms (-1, -1, -0.1), `M + 0x80` / `M + 0x94` =
their inverses (0.7673, 0.4316), `M - 0x98` = aspect 1.7778. **The FOV setting (`FOV="75"` in
`Documents/My Games/Far Cry 6/gamerprofile.xml`) is horizontal.** Screen position of a world
point: `d = p - eye`, `x = (d·right)/(d·fwd) * sx`, `y = (d·up)/(d·fwd) * sy`, pixel
`((1 + x) W/2, (1 - y) H/2)`. Checked with `tools/esp.py`: a 438-bone skeleton drawn over a
civilian 9 m away matched his body. The first chains found
(`0x6bef3b0 -> 0x118 -> 0x260` and friends) pointed into temporary pools and broke on restart.
Still to do: replace them with an AOB signature of the code that reads the static pointer, so a
game update doesn't break them.

## Aim-assist target list (the game's own list of characters)
Found 2026-10-05 while the player stood still near an outpost. The aim-assist system keeps an array of
"aiming points", one per nearby character (the player is entry 0):

```
aimAssist = [FC_m64d3d12.dll + 0x6e3d2d0]     vtable +0x5bb5788 (near "SnapHelper", "FollowHelper",
                                              "ShootCorrectionHelper", "FocusTarget")
aimAssist + 0x30 : pointer to SAimingPoint* array
aimAssist + 0x38 : u32 (0x1e seen)   aimAssist + 0x3c : u32 count (16 seen)
point + 0x00 : vtable +0x5bb2638 (near "SAimingPoint", "CAimingComponentData")
point + 0x08 : entity handle {u64 id, entity*}
point + 0x30 : R44 world transform of the aiming point (~0.85 m above the feet)

entity + 0x00 : vtable +0x5c80b80 for humans (the dog had +0x596b070)
entity + 0x18 : archetype name {u32 len, u32, u32 1, char[len]}
entity + 0x20 : back pointer to the handle
entity + 0x28 : component, vtable +0x59430b8
entity + 0x30 : R44 world transform of the entity
entity + 0xa8 : array of component pointers (vtables +0x5c79c10, +0x5951dd8, +0x59523b8, +0x5953d78)
```
Archetype names seen at the outpost: `PawnPlayer.PawnPlayer_Tetra` (player),
`NPC.Assaulter.Cuban_Hostile[.Female]`, `NPC.Pistolero.Cuban_Hostile[.Female]` (soldiers),
`NPC.Unarmed.Cuban_Neutral.Civilian[.Female]`, `Animals:Ground:Dog_BASE.Dog_Domestic`,
`NPC.Assaulter.Characters.CHAR013` (a named character). `_Hostile` in the name is a first enemy
filter; the live faction/hostility field is still to find.
`python tools/targets.py` prints the list live. Entries past `count` are stale copies.
Other static roots to the same object: `[0x69d4530] -> +0x4c8 -> +0x720` and similar (the owner of
those chains keeps the manager at `+0x720`).
**Verified after a game restart** (2026-10-05): same global, same layout, new heap addresses.
The player is not always entry 0: identify it by `PawnPlayer` in the archetype name.

## Character components (signature)
Every character (player and NPCs) carries the same 9 transforms: `584b9f8+20`, `584ba00+20`,
`584ba00+70`, `59430b8+30`, `5951dc8+1a0`, `596b070+30`, `5bb2638+30` (aiming point),
`5c80b80+30`, `5f64610+40`. `584ba00` alone is a generic scene node (props too).
Next: health, live faction/hostility, skeleton bones (probably in the `+0xa8` components).

## View angles and aiming input (2026-10-05)
- Convention: `yaw = atan2(-fwd.x, fwd.y)`, `pitch = asin(fwd.z)`, radians, from the camera
  matrix forward row.
- 9 heap copies of `{pitch, roll, yaw}` floats exist, but they are outputs recomputed every
  frame: writing them does not turn the camera. The input source is not found (not needed).
- Aiming therefore uses relative mouse input (`SendInput`, `tools/mouse.py`). Calibration
  (`tools/calibrate.py`): 806 mouse counts per radian on both axes at the sensitivity used for testing;
  moving right lowers yaw, moving down lowers pitch (both factors negative).
- `tools/aim.py`: hold the aim key (right mouse by default), pulls toward the hostile head
  closest to the crosshair inside a 12 degree cone.

### View input: found (2026-10-06)
**The view angle accumulator is the pawn component (+0x5d53d88) + 0x2f20**: `+0x10` pitch, `+0x18`
yaw relative to the body (camera yaw = body yaw + it, wrapped to +-pi), radians, same signs as
the camera's `atan2(-fwd.x, fwd.y)` / `asin(fwd.z)`. Writing +0.05 turned the camera by exactly
+2.86 deg on each axis, and the value stays. The DLL aimbot writes it (`game::addViewAngles`,
`[aim] direct`, menu "Direct view (no mouse)"), SendInput stays as the fallback.
Found from the input side, with read watches while a script moved the mouse:
- The engine reads the mouse with **DirectInput8** (`GetDeviceData`, buffered; hooked by the
  Ubisoft and Steam overlays): keyboard reader `+0x37b540`, mouse reader `+0x37b7b0` (DIMOFS_X/Y
  become events with control id 9 / 10 and the delta as a float).
- Events go to a queue: `[[+0x6a5bdc0] + 0x1d0]` (vector of 0x28-byte events, value at +0x10,
  control id at +0x1c), pushed by `+0x2e9a10`, popped by `+0x2fdab0` in the input mapper loop
  (`+0x10d0240`, input context in r15).
- Input context: per-frame look action values at `+0x27c` / `+0x2b4` (+-0.088 per frame for 4
  counts), raw at `+0xf4`.
- Look controller (an object inside the pawn block, pawn + 0x4e88): `+0x2445e8e` copies the look
  action into `+0x184` (x) / `+0x188` (y); `+0x24d0580` reads them (axis inversion), adds the
  offsets at `+0xb4` and `+0x1b0` (recoil...) and calls the integrator **`+0x228df90(T, &delta,
  &look, dt)`**, which does `[T + 0x10..0x18] += delta` (T from `+0x23d7170`, = pawn + 0x2f20).
- `tools/dinput_probe.py` (DirectInput method addresses, same in every process), `watch.py
  --exec <absolute> --now`.

Dead ends on the way (all per-frame outputs: written values are overwritten and the camera
doesn't move; `tools/view_write.py`, `tools/view_hunt.py --test`):
- `tools/view_hunt.py` (snapshots at rest / after a mouse turn right / down; fields whose changes
  follow the camera's, any absolute value or unit): pawn component `S` (+0x5d53d88) `+0x2d44` is a
  forward vector (its z passes for the pitch), struct `S + 0x2d38` written by `+0x1f3afb4`.
- Pawn data `P = [S + 0x2ae8]`, `P + 0x330` = world orientation quaternion of a node attached to
  a bone: `+0x23ca470` (per-frame cache stamped at `P + 0x1d0`) -> `+0x23c9b40` = entity rotation
  (`+0xff42e0`) x skeleton bone rotation (`+0xb402f0`, bone index at node + 0x20): the camera
  follows the animated head.
- Camera object `q = [[+0x6a66ea0] + 0x2f8]` (matrix at `q + 0x260`, written on a render thread by
  `+0x439520` from `q + 0x3d0`): `q + 0x6a0..0x6a8` = view angles (+0x6a8 = wrapped yaw), copied
  each frame by `+0x736200` from a camera controller struct `C + 0xb0..0xb8` (C + 0x70 = dirty),
  which is set by `+0x40dfa0` (SetAngles) from `+0x12ebe8f`. That function (`+0x12eb000`, large)
  derives the angles from an eye position and a look-at point (`+0x12d1ee0`), subtracts two
  offset vectors (recoil / sway?) and runs a camera collision query (filter 0x13). `q + 0x398` is
  not an angle (it decreases while the view turns: a clock?).
- Next if resumed: climb from `+0x12eb000`'s look-at inputs (or the animation's aim parameters)
  to the value the mouse accumulates.

## Line of sight (in progress)
- No ready-made "visible" list in the aim-assist manager (only the target array at `+0x30`).
- Render-counter hunt (fields that tick only for on-screen characters) found nothing reliable;
  in-game ground truth was too weak to conclude (2 visible, 2 far away).
- Physics is **Havok hknp** (`hknpWorld`, `hknpCompressedMeshShape`, `hknpCapsuleShape`...),
  plus older `hkp*` shapes. The game has `LineOfFire`, `LineOfFireInfo` and many `Raycast*`
  parameters. Plan: call the game's own physics ray cast from an internal DLL (offline only),
  with the bullet collision filter, so the game decides what blocks a shot.
- Found through Havok profiler tags (`Tt*` strings loaded with `lea` inside the functions):
  - `+0x48983a0` = **hknpWorld::castRay(world, const hknpRayCastQuery&, collector)** (tag
    "TtWorldCastRay"). It forwards to `[[world+0x4b0]]->vtbl[0x90](iface, query, world+0x28,
    collector, 0)`. Two direct callers: `+0x48651ea`, `+0x486d3f0`.
  - `+0x4865130` = wrapper `(rcx: filter source, u32 at +0x1c = collision filter info;
    rdx: {from at +0x30, to at +0x40}; r8: hknpWorld; r9: collector)`. It builds the query on
    the stack: filter = `world+0xa78`, shape-tag codec = `world+0x4a8`, materialId 0xffff,
    level of detail 2, flags 0xfb, ray origin/direction/inverse direction. Only referenced from
    a vtable at `+0x618b7e8` (slot 11), no live instance found yet.
  - `+0x4792xxx` "CollQueryWorldRayCast" = the engine's batched (job) query path, harder to call.
  - Lua-exposed names exist: `GetBonePosition` (lea at `+0x35f90f8`), `IsEntityInRay`
    (`+0x44a92d1`), `LineOfSight` (`+0x36d692c`).
- Active ragdoll bodies hold their Havok body id at `body+0x13c` (e.g. 2282, 3700).
- Second caller `+0x486d280` builds everything on its stack, which `src/internal/dllmain.cpp`
  mirrors: query (0x70 bytes: filter 0, codec 0, materialId 0xffff, filterInfo 0, LOD 2,
  flags 0xfb, origin at +0x30, direction (to - from, w = 1) at +0x40, inverse direction at +0x50)
  and a closest-hit collector (0xa0 bytes, vtable `+0x6197548`, early-out 1.8e19 at +0x10,
  fraction FLT_MAX at +0x50, hit count at +0x0c, hit position +0x30, normal +0x40, hit body id
  at +0x78 & 0xffffff). `world + 0x28` = hknpBody array (stride 0xb0, position at +0x30).
- Finding the world by heap structure failed (too many look-alikes), and hknpWorld::castRay is
  never called in normal play (30 s with a hardware breakpoint on it: no hit).
- **Working path (2026-10-06): record and replay of the game's per-ray query.**
  - The game's frequent ray queries go through `+0x47921c0(queryObject, entry, collector,
    secondary)`. `queryObject` (vtable `+0x613bad8`) is the engine broadphase; its method
    `+0xc8` walks 4 layers (`queryObject+0x70`, stride 0x30) with `+0x47152b0`.
  - Entry (0x50): from +0x00, to +0x10, flag +0x20, collision filter info +0x24 (the captured
    game query uses `0x10c3b`), results pointer +0x30, capacity +0x38, count +0x3c, secondary
    flag +0x40. Results are 0x60 bytes each, hit fraction at +0x10 (1.0 = nothing). With
    capacity 1 the collector keeps the closest hit.
  - Collector (0x90, vtable `+0x614c018`): method 1 `+0x4791ee0` is the hit callback; it writes
    results into the entry's array (count/capacity at entry+0x3c/+0x38).
  - Havok code needs a per-thread memory router (`TlsGetValue([+0x7095a98])`, slot 12), whose
    first member is an `hkLifoAllocator` (+0x10 slab size, +0x18 cur, +0x20 end, +0x28 first
    non-LIFO end). Foreign threads have none.
  - `fc6internal.dll`: hardware execute breakpoint (Dr0, no code patch) on `+0x47921c0`, copies
    the entry, the collector and the thread's router on the first call; then gives its own
    worker thread that router with a private 16 MB LIFO stack, and serves rays by calling
    `+0x47921c0` with the copies, its own from/to and a capacity-1 result array.
  - Checked in game: ground 1.62 m below the eye, nothing towards the sky, wall 2.40 m ahead;
    6 rays in 0.9 ms. `tools/rays.py` (client), `tools/visibility.py` (per-enemy report).
  - Validated in game at an outpost: verdicts matched what the player saw for 3 enemies (one fully
    visible, two hidden). A tagged enemy is drawn through walls by the game, so it can't be
    checked by eye. The captured filter (`0x10c3b`) comes from an arbitrary game query (maybe AI
    sight); if bullets ever disagree, capture the filter while the player fires.
- `tools/disasm.py` disassembles the dump with llvm-objdump and annotates strings.

## Ragdoll bodies = hit zones (found 2026-10-05)
Each character has a 19-body ragdoll (Havok-style rigid bodies). When the ragdoll is active the
bodies follow the animation, so they give exact world positions for head, neck, spine, limbs:

```
phys  = component with vtable +0x5933868 in entity + 0xa8
X     = [[phys + 0x70] + 0x08]      ragdoll instance, vtable +0x58a3bd0, X + 0x10 = entity handle
C     = [[X + 0x88] + 0x128]
C + 0xd8 : body* array, C + 0xe0 : u32 count (19 for humans)
body + 0x000 : vtable +0x615f2e0
body + 0x0a0 : X (back pointer)
body + 0x0b8 : name pointer -> "agdoll_Head" (the leading 'R' sits one byte before)
body + 0x13c : u32 physics-world index, 0xffffffff = inactive
body + 0x150 : vtable +0x615d148 / +0x615d610 / +0x615d880 (Pelvis, Spine)
body + 0x170 : rotation, 3 columns of 16 bytes;  body + 0x1a0 : world position
```
Human order: Pelvis, L Thigh, L Calf, L Foot, R Thigh, R Calf, R Foot, Spine, Spine1,
LeftShoulder, L UpperArm, L Forearm, L Hand, Neck, Head, RightShoulder, R UpperArm, R Forearm,
R Hand. Heights above the feet on a standing soldier: feet 0.06, calves 0.48, thighs 0.91,
spine1 1.10, shoulders 1.40, neck 1.47, head 1.59 (body origin = base of the skull).

Caveats, seen at the outpost: only 1 enemy out of 8 had an active ragdoll (18 m away); the
player's ragdoll is inactive (model-space values); one "active" ragdoll sat 41 m away from its
entity. `tools/targets.py` therefore uses the ragdoll only when it is active **and** its pelvis is
within 1 m of the entity, otherwise it estimates the head at 1.6 m above the entity.

## Animated skeleton (2026-10-06)
`tools/skeleton.py`. Skeleton instance `S = [[entity + 0x28] + 0xe0]` (vtable `+0x5951dc8`):
- `S + 0x20`: bone definitions, stride 0x30 (bind quat +0x00, bind position +0x10, **parent
  index int16 at +0x24**, -1 for the root); `S + 0x38`: u32 bone count (97 for human NPCs:
  spine, neck, head and face; no arms or legs, it looks like the head/face rig).
- `S + 0xe0`: bone name table `{u32 crc32(name), u32 index}` sorted by hash, count at `S + 0xec`
  (428 entries). Names are plain CRC32 (zlib): Root 1, Hips 4, Spine 5, Spine1 6, Spine2 7,
  Neck 8, Head 9 (base of the skull), HeadEnd 10 (top of the head).
- `S + 0x4b8`: u32 number of animated bones (0 when asleep, small numbers for animation LOD).
- `S + 0x4d8`: animated pose, parent-relative, stride 0x20 (quat +0x00, position +0x10).
- `S + 0x4f8`: animated pose **in model space**, same layout: used directly, no composition.
- Aim points: head = middle of Head and HeadEnd, neck, chest (Spine2), pelvis (Hips). Shape
  check: head at least 0.35 m above the pelvis (rejects stale poses and bodies on the ground).
- Coverage at the outpost: 4 of 9 hostiles had a usable pose (stable over 200 reads); others
  had no pose (one only 15 m away) or only 5 animated bones. `aim_points()` then falls back to
  the active ragdoll, then to fixed heights.
- That 97-bone instance is a head/face rig. **Full-body skeleton (found 2026-10-06, Berserker
  3 m in front of the player while his rig had 0 animated bones):**
  - body component = the component with vtable `+0x595f6e8` in `entity + 0xa8`
  - `+0x1c0`: skeleton instance (vtable `+0x5951dc8`) carrying the crc32 name table
  - `+0x1d8`: model-space pose, stride 0x20, position at +0x10; `+0x1e8`: u32 bone count
    (428 for human NPCs, up to 541 seen; Hips 4, Neck 8, Head 9, HeadEnd 10, RightArm 236,
    RightHand 246, LeftArm 303, LeftHand 313, RightFoot 395, LeftFoot 411 on the soldiers)
  - `+0x1c8`, `+0x1d0`, `+0x1e0`: other pose buffers (not used yet)
  - Some models use other indices (one "Outcast" has Head 30, no HeadEnd) or LOD skeletons
    (107 bones, no Spine2). `aim_bones()` looks bones up by name, makes HeadEnd/Spine2
    optional, and checks anatomy (pelvis < neck < head with human-sized gaps).
  - Off-screen NPCs have no animated pose: harmless, they are never inside the aim cone.
- Aim source order in `targets.aim_points()`: full-body pose, head rig, active ragdoll,
  animated pelvis (+0.73 m for the head), fixed heights.

### Skeleton ownership — the correct read (2026-10-06, supersedes the notes above and below)
Everything above that reads `body + 0x1c0 .. 0x1e8` or `[S + 0x4f8]` put skeletons on the wrong
NPCs (seen with `tools/esp.py`). Found with the write watch (`tools/watch.py`):
- The character's own skeleton instance `S` (vtable `+0x5951dc8`) is the same pointer at
  `[[entity + 0x28] + 0xe0]`, `[body + 0xa0]` and `[comp9 + 0x40]` (three links that agree).
  `[[entity + 0x28] + 0xe0]` is sometimes null (3 civilians of 15); `[body + 0xa0]` still
  holds a valid skeleton then (458/438/438 bones, claimed by no other NPC), so it is the
  fallback. With it, all 15 NPCs from 2 m to 274 m had a sound pose.
- The animation job publishes through `+0xb22120(dst, src, onlyMasked)` into
  `dst + 0x68` (local pose), `dst + 0x78` (**model-space pose**) and `dst + 0x88` (mask). With
  `dst = S` this is the character's full pose; the bone count is the name-table count
  `S + 0xec`, and the buffers are back to back (`[S+0x78] - [S+0x68] = count * 0x20`):
  428/428, 438/438, 446/446 on every NPC checked. Names from `S + 0xe0` (Hips 4, Neck 8,
  Head 9, HeadEnd 10 on the human models).
- `body + 0x1c0` points to **another** character's skeleton (chains seen: civilian → civilian
  → ally) and `body + 0x1c8 .. 0x1e0` are that other skeleton's `+0x68/0x78/0x88` buffers,
  `body + 0x1e8` its count. That is why poses looked swapped, stale or zigzag.
- `[S + 0x4e8 / 0x4f8]` (count `S + 0x4b8`, 162 near, fewer far) is a second output written
  by the same function with `dst = S + 0x480`; its bone order does not follow the name table
  (HeadEnd == Neck), so it is not used.
- With the correct read, 11 of 12 NPCs from 3 m to 246 m (on and off screen) gave anatomically
  sound poses, including a crouching enemy (Hips 0.26, Head 0.83) and CHAR014, who had
  "5 bones" through the wrong link.

### Allies and civilians as test subjects (2026-10-06)
- Factions are archetype suffixes on the **same NPC classes**: `NPC.Assaulter.Cuban_Hostile`
  (enemy), `NPC.Assaulter.Cuban_Friendly[.Female]` (guerrilla ally), `NPC.Unarmed.Cuban_Neutral.*`
  (civilians, farmers, Outcast). Same body component, same 428-438 bone skeleton, same indices
  (Hips 4, Neck 8, Head 9, HeadEnd 10): what works on an ally works on an enemy.
- Standing NPC, model space: feet 0.07, Hips 0.89-0.90, Spine2 1.20, Neck 1.46-1.48,
  Head 1.57-1.58, HeadEnd 1.70-1.73, highest skull/face bone 1.71-1.76 (higher bones are hats
  or gear). So the Head/HeadEnd midpoint (~1.64) is the middle of the skull.
- Body pose bone counts seen: 428/431/437/438 (full body), **190** (another model family:
  Hips 2, Spine2 19, Neck 20, Neck1 21, Head 22, no HeadEnd; seen on a sitting/lying civilian,
  a hostage and an enemy scout), **5** (a placeholder pose 0.18 m tall with other bone names:
  animation LOD for distant or culled NPCs, even at 8 m when off-screen), 0 (no pose).
- The vertical anatomy check rejects sitting, kneeling and lying poses (190-bone civilian:
  Hips 0.19, Head 0.48), which then fall back to "1.6 m above the feet", i.e. into the air.
- The 5-bone state lives in the same (only) body component, which switches between 5 and
  428-438 bones: a crowd/LOD mode with no CPU skeleton. Seen on a farmer 36 m away in plain
  view, and on enemies around 200 m. In that mode the head rig has 0 animated bones and the
  aim-assist point (`point + 0x30`) sits at the entity's feet, so nothing gives the head.
- **The body pose is often stale or mis-indexed (ESP captures, 2026-10-06):**
  - Stale: the closest NPC (7.7 m) had body `+0x1d0/+0x1d8/+0x1e0` frozen for 2 s while its
    `entity+0x28` rig buffers changed every frame; farther NPCs show the opposite. A farmer who
    stood up kept his crouched pose, one who crouched kept a standing pose; dead NPCs keep
    their last standing pose (the ragdoll takes over). The game pauses without focus, so
    freshness can only be measured while it has focus.
  - Wrong table: the body's own name table (`[body+0x1c0] + 0xe0`) always has exactly the
    body's bone count (190/190, 304/304, 431/431, 438/438). When `+0x1c0` is null, the rig's
    table (428 or 431 entries) was used instead and mapped names to wrong bones.
  - The 304-bone model gives nonsense even with its own table (Hips 1.41, Neck 1.43,
    Head 2.19): its buffer or indices follow another layout.
  - Some "rig" and "inst" buffers hold identical Hips/Neck/Head values on different NPCs
    (0.83/1.33/1.44/1.60 and 0.52/1.46/1.44/1.43): reference poses, not live ones.
  - `CBTTaskPawnAgent_GetBonePosition` (name `+0x5fc50d8`, fields `selOperation`, `EntityId`,
    `selBone`, `OutFact`; data object vtable `+0x5fc5100`, factory `+0x3fbbb90`): its method at
    slot 6 jumps into VMProtect-virtualized code (`jmp +0x1c011630`), so it can't be read
    statically. `tools/xrefs.py` finds strings and RIP-relative references in the dump.
- **Pose publication, found with a hardware write watch (`tools/watch.py`, 2026-10-06):** for a
  farmer 6.7 m away, the Head entry of `[S + 0x4f8]` (S = `[[entity + 0x28] + 0xe0]`, 162 bones
  published) was written 279 times in 1.5 s by:
  - `+0xb22120(dst = S + 0x480, src, onlyMasked)`: copies the animation job's result into the
    skeleton instance under a spin lock at `S + 0x598` (readers count `S + 0x59c`):
    `src+0x08` → `[S + 0x4e8]` (local pose), `src+0x10` → `[S + 0x4f8]` (model pose),
    `src+0x18` = per-bone mask copied to `[S + 0x508]`, `src+0x20` = bone count. Called from
    `+0xb884f0(job, dst)` with `src = [job + 0xb8] + 0x30`. With `onlyMasked`, only bones whose
    mask byte has bits 0-1 set are copied.
  - `+0xb60596` (another thread) also writes `[S + 0x4f8]` (not read yet).
  - So `[S + 0x4f8]` with `[S + 0x4b8]` bones is the published model pose; the name table of S
    (428/431 entries) indexes the full skeleton, and LOD publishes a prefix (Head 9 of 162).
  - That farmer's body component had no `+0x1c0` instance and a stale `+0x1d8` buffer.
- `tools/esp.py`: click-through overlay drawing every NPC's body skeleton and the aim point
  the aimbot would use (green skeleton, yellow ragdoll/pelvis estimate, red fixed height).
  F9 saves `captures/esp_*.png` + `.json`.
- `tools/skeleton_lab.py` prints, per NPC, the faction, bone count, aim source and why the
  checks passed or failed; `--aim` prints how far each computed head point is from the
  crosshair (cm right/up at the target's distance) for the NPC closest to it.

## Faction and attitude (2026-10-06)
Found with `tools/find_faction.py` (fields equal within each archetype group, different between
enemies and allies): **u32 at `+0x2c` of the component with vtable `+0x5bf9c58`** (in
`entity + 0xa8`; strings around the vtable: `StatValues`, `Grouping`, `LevelChangedEvent`,
`BerserkMaxDifferenceInLevels`, `bForceLevel`, the level/rank system).
- It is the **attitude toward the player, live**, not a fixed faction: 4 = Libertad (allies,
  Guapo the amigo, the player's vehicles), 2 = hostile right now, 1 = neutral. During a fight
  every `Cuban_Hostile` soldier was 2 (also `CHAR125_Guerrilla_Scout_PlayGo_Male`, no
  `_Hostile` suffix); once things calmed down three living soldiers were 1, like civilians and
  a captured ally (`ENC_HostageAssaulter`).
- Vehicles and amigos carry it too; the player entity has no such component.
- No clean membership field found yet. Candidates: component `+0x5bd96b0` has
  `{0xee5fa2cc, 11}` at `+0x50c` and `{0x9da650a4, 11}` at `+0x518` on every soldier, `{0, -1}`
  on allies and civilians (strings near its vtable are about flares: soldiers' signal flares?).
- `targets.is_hostile()`: humans only; never attitude 4; attitude 2, or `_Hostile` in the
  archetype name (soldiers not alerted yet, for stealth).
- Attitude 5 (2026-10-06, mission "Rencontre avec les Montero"): every NPC around read 5,
  guerrillas (`Cuban_Friendly`) and soldiers, alive and dead; only a civilian read 1. There the
  name decides: never `_Friendly` / `_Neutral`; `_Hostile` and FND elites (`Base.NPC.Elite.*`,
  e.g. `Elite.AR_RPG`, no faction suffix, previously drawn grey) are enemies.

### NPC AI target: who is after the player (2026-10-06)
- First try, `tools/threat_hunt.py`: every field of each NPC's components (and their pointees)
  that holds the player (entity, component or entity handle), logged through a fight. Too noisy:
  every soldier already referenced the player in 11-98 places while calm (perception), and the
  mission set every attitude to 5, so nothing separated the ones fighting.
- Read instead from the code that aims NPC weapons: the fire direction `+0x2848c40` takes
  `+0x28485f0` for non-player shooters. With `[X + 0x48]` it fires at the scripted point
  `X + 0x20`; else it asks the shooter's pawn for its AI aim data
  **`A = [pawn + 0x2ae8] + 0xd40`** (`+0x23d1dd0`): `A + 0x34` aim point, **`[A + 0xe8]` entity
  handle of the target** (`[handle + 8]` = entity), `[A + 0xf0]` bone and `[A + 0xf4]` time to
  track it (seen -1: unused, the aim point is used).
- NPC pawn component: vtable **`+0x5fcd820`** (the player's is `+0x5d53d88`). NPC weapons: the
  inventory component `+0x5ccc300` holds the weapon object at `+0x6e8` (`AutoPistols.APS._NPC`,
  `Rifles.AKC_L._NPC`, `Launchers.RPG7_Classic`), with the same fire component layout.
- `tools/npc_targets.py` during a fight: a soldier's target switched between the player, a
  crocodile, a guerrilla and a dog; guerrillas targeted soldiers; NPCs not fighting had none.
- `game::aiTarget()`; the aimbot takes the NPCs targeting the player first (`[aim] threats_first`,
  score x0.25), then the alerted ones; the ESP writes "TE VISE" above them and the HUD target card
  says "te vise". Confirmed in a fight: "TE VISE" shows exactly on the soldiers shooting at
  the player.

## Health and death (2026-10-06)
Settled with `tools/life_log.py`, which logs every change of the candidate fields while the player
wounded then killed soldiers and wounded a captured ally:
- **Death: u32 `+0x50` of the component with vtable `+0x59336e8`**: 0 while alive (wounded
  included), then 1 -> 2 -> 3 within ~2 s as the character dies. At the same moment `+0x54`
  (3, 4, 6, 8 seen: hit part?) and `+0x98` (1 then 2) become non-zero, as does `+0x9c` of the
  ragdoll component `+0x5933868` (1 -> 2 -> 3), and the head drops to the ground.
  Timeline: a Pistolero wounded at 4.2 s (only `+0x298` changed), killed at 9.7 s (flags);
  another killed with one shot at 17.1 s (flags at once); the wounded ally kept all flags at 0.
- Damage component `+0x5a74d68` (strings near its vtable: `fHealthPercentThreshold`,
  `OnDeathStimData`, `SetInvincible`, `SMaxHealthMultiplier`): `+0x298` drops from 50 to 0 at
  the **first wound** and `+0x29c` stays 50 (not the health).
- Not health either: `+0x60` of `+0x59336e8` (500, sometimes 0 after death); `+0x2d0` of
  component `+0x5951dd8` (1.0, 0.9 on several NPCs at the same instant, wounded or not).
- **Health: `[[damage component + 0x48] + 0x18]`** (float, current) and `+0x1c` (max), in a
  health object with vtable `+0x5a49c68`. Found with `tools/hp_hunt.py` (snapshots of one NPC
  between shots, sub-objects included, minus fields that moved on a control NPC): 81.8 -> 63.6
  -> 45.4 -> -243.2 on a captured ally shot in the legs then killed (18.2 per leg shot; the first
  value was already 100 - 18.2). 100/100 on every healthy NPC seen, negative on every dead one
  (-18, -20.7, -43, -243.2). Earlier diffs missed it because it sits behind a pointer.
- A dead body's own skeleton pose follows the body to the ground and stops changing once it
  settles (`skeleton.select_pose()` reports it frozen).
- `targets.is_dead()` (death state non-zero, or health <= 0) removes dead NPCs from `aim.py`
  and `visibility.py`; the ESP prints the health (`82/100hp`) or `DEAD`.

## Ballistics (2026-10-06)
Measured, not guessed: `fc6internal.dll` v7 has a **trace** mode (Dr0 on the per-ray query
`+0x47921c0` stays armed; every query with a given filter is logged with from/to,
QueryPerformanceCounter time, thread and return address). `tools/ballistics.py` starts it on F8,
logs the camera and the left button, then chains segments whose start is the previous end.
- A bullet is a **projectile**: one bullet-filter (`0x11fbb`) query per frame, from its previous
  position to its next one (~5 m per 8 ms frame), within 2-9 ms of the click. All queries come
  through the dispatcher at `+0x47923e0` (the return address doesn't tell systems apart).
- It starts **exactly at the camera position, along the camera forward** (0.00 m and
  0.00-0.01 deg on 6 shots): no muzzle offset, no spread on single shots.
- **Constant speed ~614 m/s** (first half = second half of a 0.46 s flight: no drag), and a
  **perfect parabola** (residual < 1 mm over 287 m) with **gravity ~16.5 m/s2** (16.38-16.65 on
  6 shots; `g = -2 c v^2` from the z(x) curvature, which doesn't depend on frame timing).
- It disappears after **~288 m** of path (287.5-288.0 on 9 shots at the sky).
- Weapon of the capture: the **FAL**. Other weapons, measured live by `aim.py` (see below):
  497-498 m/s / 14.8-14.9 m/s2, 379-380 m/s / 15.15 m/s2, and a sniper at ~37 m per frame
  (over 4 km/s, gravity 14-16 but barely measurable: the drop is a few cm over 220 m).
- `aim.py` aims at `point + velocity * t + (0, 0, g t^2 / 2)` with `t = |aim - eye| / speed`
  (3 iterations), the velocity being the target root's over the last 0.1 s. Simulated against
  the model: < 3 mm miss up to 200 m. Orders of magnitude: drop 5.5 cm at 50 m, 22 cm at 100 m,
  88 cm at 200 m; lead for a 5 m/s run 41 cm at 50 m, 81 cm at 100 m.

### Weapon fields (static), and why the values are measured instead
- Reflected field names (description strings next to them in .rdata): `bUseBulletPhysic`
  +0x324, **`fBulletSpeed` +0x328** ("Speed of the bullet (m/s)"), **`fBulletGravity` +0x32c**,
  **`fBulletDropDistance` +0x330** ("Distance before the gravity starts to be applied"),
  `fBulletUnderwaterDeceleration` +0x334, start speed +0x338; same struct: spread
  `fAngleYawBulletSpread` +0x2b4 / `fAnglePitchBulletSpread` +0x2b8, `iBurstLength` +0x2ac,
  `RangeMultiplier*` lists +0x228. Registered by `+0x29adc40` (base) for a derived class
  registered by `+0x29b60a0`. Modifiers elsewhere: `fBulletSpeedMultiplier`,
  `fBulletGravityMultiplier`, `fBulletDropDistanceMultiplier` ("Multiplies the bullet ...
  parameter on the weapon", `+0x15ceb37`).
- (Superseded by "Effective ballistics: solved" below.) Not solved then: the instance for the held weapon. The vtable right after the
  `CWeaponFireBulletProperties` name (`+0x5a27fb8`, 3945 instances) doesn't have these fields at
  +0x324; floats near 614 with a physics flag gave nothing convincing; 9 code sites that read
  +0x328 and +0x32c together (`tools/watch.py --exec`, DLL v8 execute breakpoints) never ran
  while the player fired (two run every frame on unrelated objects).
- Bullet queries are batched and run as **jobs**: dispatcher `+0x47923e0` (walks `[rsi+0x38]`,
  count `[rsi+0x40]`, entries of 0x50 on the worker's stack) <- `+0x4644163` <- `+0x9e5afd`
  <- job system `+0x26e669`. The results pointer (+0x30) is in a separate pool. So neither the
  call stack (DLL v10 snapshots with unwound registers) nor the results lead to the bullet.
- Measuring is exact anyway and includes attachments, ammo and difficulty: **DLL v11 bullet
  monitor** keeps the trace armed (ring buffer, heartbeat from the client, re-armed every 5 s for
  new threads). `ballistics.BulletMonitor` chains the segments, keeps trajectories that start at
  the eye, and `estimate()` gives speed (path / flight time) and gravity (curvature of z(x) times
  v^2). `aim.py` recalibrates after each of the player's shots (15 m of flight for the speed, 40 m
  for the gravity; close values averaged, a new weapon taken as is). Cost: our own ray round
  trip 0.022 -> 0.032 ms with the monitor on.

### Held weapon (found with `tools/weapon_hunt.py`)
- Snapshots of the player's entity, components and their pointees while holding FAL, SR-A Yaran,
  FAL, SR-A Yaran: the component with vtable `+0x5bb55e8` (strings around it: `CAimingAssist*`,
  `CMarksmanParams`) holds the **held weapon object** at `+0x248` (same at `+0x358`, `+0x548`),
  vtable `+0x5bd8f20`. `[weapon + 0x8]` is a handle: `[[weapon + 0x8] + 0x8]` = the weapon
  entity, whose archetype names it: `weapons:Weapons:Rifles.FAL`,
  `weapons:Weapons:Rifles.CUBAN-SRA` (the SR-A Yaran). `[weapon + 0xb0]` -> owner (the player).
  The player's entity is in the aim-assist list (`player:MainCharacter/PawnPlayer...`).
- Sub-objects of the weapon object: `CWeaponTrackingProperties` (`+0x5c59f98`),
  `CWeaponAnimationData` (`+0x5c58678`), `CWeaponAnimation`, `CWeaponControllerNetObject`.
  Bullet speed/gravity were not found within 4 levels of pointers (30000 objects, 64 KB windows)
  nor in the weapon entity's 32 components. Dead ends: records holding
  `... 0.15 0.15 0.5 0 25 0.05 500 15 1 15 2 ...` looked like SR-A stats but a static pointer
  (`+0x6a6a9f8`) shows they are `CVectorDisplacementWaveFormObject` (sea waves; the mission was at
  sea); of the 9 routines that copy +0x328/+0x32c/+0x330 as a block, only `+0x57e797` ran during
  weapon switches and shots, on another struct (job-system caller `+0x5d2cf6`).
- **Weapon stats found** (anchored on the menu's numbers, "Cadence de tir 700 bpm", "Chargeur 20"
  for the FAL): the weapon object holds the fire rate in rpm at `+0x170` (FAL 700, MS16 S 650) and
  the magazine at `+0x188` (FAL 20). Its fire properties, the reflected struct whose field offsets
  were known (`+0x29adc40`), are at `[[weapon + 0x168] + 0x10]` (vtable `+0x5c59958`, under
  `CWeaponTrackingProperties`): `bUseBulletPhysic` +0x324 = 1, **`fBulletSpeed` +0x328** (FAL 615,
  MS16 S 560), **`fBulletGravity` +0x32c = -15** (stored negative), `fBulletDropDistance` +0x330 =
  0, underwater deceleration 500, start speed 100. `SupportedAmmoList` +0x6f0 (7 entries of 0xd8,
  damage factors per target type, not ballistics), `iDefaultAmmoIndex` +0x700.
- The FAL measured 613-614 m/s (game 615) but fell at 16.45 m/s2 (game 15): x1.1, from its
  attachment. Attachment properties carry `FireRangeMultiplier` +0x2b0,
  `fBulletSpeedMultiplier` +0x2b8, `fBulletGravityMultiplier` +0x2bc,
  `fBulletDropDistanceMultiplier` +0x2c0 (registered at `+0x15ceb37`, with `baseDamagePrimary`
  and impact stims); the instance for the mounted attachment wasn't found yet.
- The MS16 S (armor-piercing rounds, makeshift suppressor, iron sights) has fBulletSpeed 560 in
  its data but its bullets measured 3567-3675 m/s on 5 shots (7 segments over 288 m): x6.5
  from a mod. The SR-A Yaran (no such mod) matches its data exactly (500 m/s, 15 m/s2).
- `tools/mod_hunt.py` (F8 with mod A, B, A, B, objects named by their pointer path from the
  weapon): switching AP / standard ammo on the MS16 S left fBulletSpeed at 560 (the multiplier
  is applied elsewhere) and only showed `[[weapon + 0x198]] + 0x620` going 9 / 10 (ammo index?);
  the first run printed only 300 of 1647 fields, it now saves its snapshots to `captures/`.
  Blind searches for a x6.5 multiplier or a ~3600 effective speed only found 2*pi constants and
  world coordinates.
- Measured effects (MS16 S, data 560 m/s / 15 m/s2, shots measured by `aim.py`; ammo switches in
  game, no workbench needed): standard ammo 447 m/s / 16.4, armor-piercing ~3600 m/s / 15.0,
  hollow point 447 m/s / 15.0. So: makeshift suppressor speed x0.8, standard ammo gravity x1.1
  (the FAL, standard ammo, no suppressor: 615 m/s / 16.45), armor-piercing speed x8, hollow
  point x1. The UI's "Vélocité" bar changes with the ammo too.
- Supported ammo of rifles (names in the descriptors `[[entry + 0x18] + 0x10]`, ids at entry
  +0x10): `Ammo/Rifle_BaseAmmo` (0x200752db72b88a), `..._SpecialAmmo_ArmorPiercing`
  (0x2007838b7636be), `_Explosive`, `_FullMetalJacket`, `_HollowPoint`, `_Incendiary`, `_Poison`.
  The fire props keep the default ammo at +0x768 (object), +0x770 (id), +0x778 (descriptor). The
  player has the ammo inventory in `[component +0x5ccc300 + 0x370]` (+0x218..+0x240, ids).
  `CGearItem` objects hold the ids at +0x8. The ammo multipliers weren't found.
- Loaded ammo not found: `tools/ammo_hunt.py` (fields holding an ammo id, entry or object, weapon
  graph and player components) saw none follow 3 switches; `mod_hunt.py` (weapon + player,
  saved in captures/) only showed `[[weapon + 0x198] + 0x620]`, which goes 10 -> 9 -> 10 within a
  second on every ammo switch: a weapon state (switching), not the type.
- `aim.py`: speed, gravity and drop distance = the game's values (`targets.weapon_ballistics`)
  times a per-weapon correction measured on the player's shots (`speed_x`, `gravity_x` in
  `tools/weapons.json`), so a weapon never fired is right at once and an attachment is learned
  on the first shot. The values are re-read every 0.25 s, so a change of stats is picked up.

### Effective ballistics: solved (2026-10-06, read watch)
The multipliers and the loaded ammo were found by **who reads fBulletSpeed**: DLL v14 adds a read
mode to the hardware data breakpoint (Dr7 RW = 11), `tools/watch.py --weapon 0x328 --reads` puts it
on the held weapon's own `[[weapon + 0x168] + 0x10] + 0x328` and the player fires. Three engine
instructions in `+0x284ae60` (fire setup, 20 reads in 20 s) read it; `+0x32c` (gravity) is read at
`+0x2879e2e` (`+0x2879da0`, bullet update). `tools/disasm.py live <offset>` disassembles the
running game (no dump needed). The function's object `X` (vtable `+0x5c43c08`) is
**`[weapon + 0x198]`** (`[X + 0x50]` = weapon object, `[X + 0x330]` = fire props).

```
ammo index = id [X + 0xf98] looked up in SupportedAmmoList (props + 0x6f0, count = (u64 [props +
             0x6f8] >> 32) & 0x7fffffff, entries 0xd8, id at +0x10); not found / invalid: iDefaultAmmoIndex
             [props + 0x700] if < count, else 0                                      (+0x27d71e0)
speed      = fBulletSpeed [props + 0x328] x [weapon + 0xecc] x entry[+0x3c]          (+0x2955120)
gravity    = fBulletGravity [props + 0x32c] x [weapon + 0xed0] x entry[+0x38]        (+0x2910f20)
drop       = fBulletDropDistance [props + 0x330] x [weapon + 0xed4]
range      = [weapon + 0xec4]   (288 m on the FAL: the measured 288 m of path)
```
- `[weapon + 0xec4 .. 0xed4]` are the attachment modifiers: the reflected fields +0x2b0 (range)
  .. +0x2c0 (drop) of `+0x15ceb37`, embedded at weapon + 0xc14 (all 1.0 on a FAL without mods; the
  makeshift suppressor should read 0.8 for the speed).
- Ammo entries (rifles): +0x30 = 1.3 for all, +0x34 = 1.0, **+0x38 gravity factor** (HollowPoint
  1.1, others 1.0), **+0x3c speed factor** (`FullMetalJacket` **8.0**, `ArmorPiercing` 1.2, others
  1.0). The round the game shows as armour-piercing ("perforantes") is the `FullMetalJacket` entry.
- So the earlier "measured effects" had standard and hollow point swapped (both keep the speed):
  the x1.1 gravity was the hollow point. FAL with FMJ: 615 x 1.0 x 8.0 = 4920 m/s (measured 4959).
- `[weapon + 0x198] + 0x620` (the 10 -> 9 -> 10 field of `mod_hunt.py`) is in the same object.
- `+0x368bb0` (called with the global `[+0x6a5eaa8]`) is the frame time (double at +0x88, 0 while
  paused), not a ballistic factor: the fire setup computes the distance per frame.
- `game.cpp heldWeapon()` and `targets.weapon_ballistics()` read all of it; the aimbot uses these
  values directly (no measured correction any more) and caps its distance at the weapon's range.

### Fire direction: the weapon's sway (2026-10-06)
- The fire code (`+0x2883683`, caller of `+0x284ae60`) first asks the fire component
  `X = [weapon + 0x198]` for the bullet's origin and direction: `X->vtable[0x340]` =
  **`+0x2848c40(X, shooter, &origin, &direction)`** (vec3 outputs). The shooter is the player's
  component with vtable **`+0x5d53d88`** (the pawn: `[S + 0x2ae8]` = pawn data P, `P + 0x10d8` =
  1 for the player, `P + 0x10` = position / orientation with a per-frame cache, `P + 0x1a44` =
  velocity, `P + 0xee8` = aim offsets). The player's path is `+0x2847ab0` (NPCs: `+0x28485f0`;
  with `[X + 0x48]` set it fires at a point given by `X + 0x20`).
- The DLL calls `+0x2848c40` itself every tick while aiming (`game::fireDirection`, SEH guarded;
  no crash seen). Origin = the eye (0.000 m). In a scope the direction loops around the camera
  forward, ~0.1 deg in yaw and pitch over a few seconds (33 cm at 190 m: the scope misses of the
  bullet monitor), and drops to ~0.01 deg while holding breath (which also zooms: x2.17 -> x2.36).
  Spikes up to 1.4 deg only during fast turns (camera matrix and fire direction read at different
  instants).
- The aimbot turns the camera so that this direction points at the aim point (offset smoothed,
  ignored above 0.6 deg): `[aim] barrel`, menu "Aim the barrel (sway)". Tested in game: clearly better
  at long range in a scope.

### Spread: why aimed shots still missed
- `aim.py` reports each shot fired while aiming: where the barrel (the bullet's first segment)
  pointed against the aim point at fire, the aim error read from the camera, and how far the
  bullet passed from the target part at the same instants. With the SR-A Yaran (~500 m/s) the
  arrival error equalled the barrel error at fire (the drop and lead model is right), while the
  camera was on the aim point: the bullet itself left off the camera forward.
- `ballistics.py` (signed bullet-vs-view offsets), SR-A Yaran standing, mouse still: aiming
  0.07-0.24 deg in every direction (8-41 cm at 100 m), hip fire 0-0.77 deg; the FAL showed none
  (0.00-0.02 deg). That is the weapon's random spread (`fAngleYawBulletSpread` /
  `fAnglePitchBulletSpread`), not something a better aim point can cancel.
- So `aim.py` measures the current weapon's spread on the shots fired while aiming (angle
  between the bullet and the camera forward) and aims at the chest when the median shot would
  land more than 10 cm from the head centre (SR-A Yaran: beyond ~53 m); `--head-only` keeps the
  head. It also adds the aim direction's own angular rate to the mouse ("follow"), so the view
  doesn't trail a moving aim point.
- Mouse while aiming: the camera turns ~0.75x of the hip-fire calibration per count even
  unmagnified, times 1 / zoom when zoomed (zoom = projection x scale / its value when not aiming;
  0.36x measured at zoom x2.17, 0.67-0.79x once only the zoom was compensated). `aim.py` scales
  the counts by 1.33 x zoom and refines it per zoom level from each response check
  (`tools/zoom_gain.json`), only when the camera turned the way the counts were sent (the
  player's own mouse moves spoiled the first table: 0.60 at zoom 1) and within -20/+25 %.
- Shot reports say `blocked at N m` when the bullet stopped before the target's range.
- (Superseded by the next section: the SR-A's "aimed spread" was the sway; its aimed spread is 0.)

### Spread: the game's own model (2026-10-06)
Found statically in a local dump (`fc6probe dump`, never shared): the reflected field names of the
fire properties (registration `+0x29b0700..`), then the code that reads them.
- Fire properties (same struct as fBulletSpeed): `BulletSpread` **+0x2e4** {min, max} (deg),
  `BulletSpread_crouch` +0x2ec, **`BulletSpread_ironSight` +0x2f4**, `BulletSpread_crouch_ironSight`
  +0x2fc, `BulletSpread_jump` +0x304, `fShooting_contraction_speed` +0x2d0 / `expansion` +0x2d4,
  `fBulletSpread_jump/movement_stabilization_time` +0x2d8 / +0x2dc, `bBulletSpread_clamp_spread`
  +0x2e0, `fBulletSpread_movement_modifier` +0x30c, `turn` +0x310, `jump` +0x314,
  `fMax_spread_bullet_number` +0x318, `fBulletSpread_blindFireModifier` +0x320,
  `fBase_spread_transition_time` +0x45c; ReflexFire block +0x33e..+0x36c. FAL: hip 2.5-4.0,
  crouch 2.0-3.5, aimed 0-0, 4 bullets to max; SR-A: hip 1.7-3.0, aimed 0-0.
- Weapon object (class with `ConsecutiveShots` +0x184, `PreparedForUse` +0xaf9,
  **`CurrentBulletSpread` +0xb10**, `currentSkin` +0xc28): `+0x286b6a0` writes each frame
  `[weapon + 0xb10]` = current spread, `+0xb14` / `+0xb18` = current max / min, `+0xb1c` = sum of
  the movement / jump / turn modifiers, times the global `[[+0x6e109f0] + 0x34]`.
  `[weapon + 0xbe8]` is the aim blend (>= 0.75 counts as aimed).
- The fire setup `+0x284ae60` calls **`+0x27a57b0(fire component, bullet index, &direction)`**
  after the fire direction (`+0x2848c40`, the sway). Without `bUseAngleSpread` (props + 0x2b1):
  ```
  s     = [weapon + 0xb10]                               (deg)
  r     = max(u1, u2) ^ [props + 0x2c4]                  (u1, u2: ran3 at fire component + 0x358; exponent 2)
  angle = r x s x 0.5                                    (deg; 0x57a7330 = 0.5)
  theta = u3 x 2 pi                                      (u3: global ran3 +0x292420)
  direction turned by angle around the forward, towards theta (quaternions, 0x57aa1f4 = deg -> rad)
  ```
  With the exponent 2, `angle / (s / 2)` is uniform in [0, 1]: the median is s / 4. With
  `bUseAngleSpread`, `fAngleYawBulletSpread` / `Pitch` (+0x2b4 / +0x2b8, +0x2bc / +0x2c0 after
  the first bullet), times `fAngleSpreadIronsightFactor` (+0x2c8) when aimed, and per-attachment
  factors.
- The random generators are Knuth's subtractive `ran3` (`+0x2900b0`, 55 entries, modulus 1e7).
  The size of the next deviation could be predicted from the weapon's own generator, not its
  direction (the global generator is shared).
- **Checked on real shots** (`tools/spread_check.py`: bullet monitor, camera and `[weapon + 0xb10]`
  just before each shot). FAL at hip, standing, walking, crouched, single shots and a burst, 39
  shots: `angle / (s / 2)` = 0.00-0.97, mean 0.47, quartiles 0.25 / 0.49 / 0.67 (uniform in
  [0, 1]: 0.25 / 0.5 / 0.75), never above 1. s grows by ~0.6-1.2 deg per shot and walking adds to
  it: 3.0 (crouched) to 6.0 deg, above the 4.0 "max" of the data. Aimed: s = 0 on all 10 shots; the
  0.01-0.09 deg left are the sway (already aimed through the barrel).
- `game::heldSpread()` reads it; the aimbot aims at the chest first when fewer than half the
  shots would hit a 10 cm head at the target's distance (`P = (atan(0.1 / d) / (s / 2)) ^ (2 / p)`),
  so aimed rifles always go for the head and hip fire switches at ~4-9 m with the FAL (s = 6 to 2.5).

## Inventory (2026-10-06): item objects found, counts protected
Goal: a menu to edit the counts of crafting materials, camp resources, tradeables, keys...
- Value scans (`fc6probe ints`, `tools/inv_hunt.py`): the counts read in the inventory screen
  (metal 75 -> 15 -> 65 -> 72 -> 78 -> 84) never stay at one plain u32 / u16 address; the hits
  that followed for a round were UI copies (records of 0x40 bytes with `vog--...` resource
  strings, updated only when the screen opens) and transient script records.
- The player's item pouch component **`+0x5ccc300`** (also the NPCs': weapon at `+0x6e8`) holds
  per-category lists of item ids `{ptr, size|cap}` at `+0x340..+0x5b0` (13 ids at `+0x4a0`,
  the materials, metal included).
- Item classes: `CInventoryItem` -> **`CInventoryItemStackable`** (type getter `+0x2adddd0`) ->
  `CInventoryItemAmmo`, `Bag`, `Medkit`, `Quest`. A stackable object: vtable `+0x5cc8600`, the
  reflection interface at `+8` (vtable **`+0x5cc8828`**, `GetType` `+0x2af8770`; casts return
  `obj - 8`), `+0x20` the owning pouch, **`+0x28` the item descriptor** (`[desc + 0x20]` = item
  id, a pointer in it to the name: `LootItem/Economic/Metal` (id `0x2007655adf62ad`),
  `LootItem/Economic/Oil`, `LootItem/Economic/Medicine`, `Money/Money`,
  `Tetra/CraftingMaterials/{TradeMaterials,AnimalParts,BlueprintMaterials,DismantlingMaterials}/...`,
  `Keys/ASLT/ASLT_OldChestKey`...), **`+0x68` the max stack** (999999, 500, 99, 1). 18 of them
  for the player (`fc6probe refs` on the interface vtable).
- **The count is not stored in clear**: `obj + 0x40` is an interface (vtable `+0x5cc8870`) over
  `{kind, offset}` slots at `+0x50 / +0x58 / +0x60` (metal: 0x4c60, 0x4c68, 0x4c70); its methods
  (e.g. `[vt + 0x18]`, called by `+0x2b0c299`) run in an unnamed executable section (`+0x1ef87000`,
  next to the protection's sections `.tls` 370 MB, `.arch`, `.xtls`). A read watch on the slot
  showed the plain count only in a register of that code (`rdi = 0x54`) next to an encoded value.
  That is the game's value protection: **not reversed, decoded or called** (no protection
  bypass, see the project's rules).
- The game's own item functions (normal code): the pouch's item tree (root `[pouch + 0x208]`,
  nodes {left +0, right +8, item id +0x20, item +0x28}; 247 items, 18 of them stackables);
  the descriptor's name at `[desc + 0x10]` (length +0, text +0xc); the pickup path
  (`+0x2abf29d -> +0x2ac0700 -> +0x2ac039c`, found with a read watch on `item + 0x40` while
  picking up metal) calls the stackable's **add `vtable +0x208` = `+0x2b0c2f0(item, n)`**
  (clamped to the max stack, returns the amount added; **remove `+0x210` = `+0x2b65300`**), which
  read the count with `+0x366cf0(item + 0x58, &count)`.
- **Calling them from the overlay thread does not work**: the count getter took so long that the
  overlay fell to 1 fps (it runs the protected code), then the game crashed (no add or remove had
  been made; the save was fine). They must not be called outside the game's own thread; the menu
  tab was removed (2026-10-06).

MSVC RTTI is stripped (`vtable[-1]` points to code), but Dunia keeps its reflection class names as
plain strings, often right before the vtables of the same source file. `fc6probe dump` writes the
unpacked module (local analysis only, never share it); about 16 000 `C*`/`S*` names are in it.
Names relevant to the aimbot:
- Aim assist built into the game: `CAimingAssist`, `CAimingAssistComponent`, `CAimingAssistSnap`,
  `CAimingAssistFollow`, `CAimingAssistMarksman`, `SLockOnAimingPointWeaponData`,
  `SBulletMagnetismConfig`, `SEnemyAimingHelper`.
- Ballistics: `CAimingBulletEvaluator`, `CAimingProjectileEvaluator`, `CAimingParabolaEvaluator`,
  `CWeaponFireBulletProperties`, `CWeaponFireBulletStrategy`, `SBulletSpread`.
- Skeleton: `CAnimSkeleton`, `CSceneSkeleton`, `CSkeletonBone`, `CAnimBone`,
  `CBTTaskPawnAgent_GetBonePosition`.
- Characters / factions: `CAgent`, `CAIFactionData`, `CPreConditionEntityFactionRelationship`,
  `CPawnEnemyMonitor`, `CAPI_NPC`, `CAPI_Player`.
Next: link names to vtables through the code that references them (constructors load both the
name and the vtable), with Ghidra on the dump.

## Method
1. `fc6probe matrices out.tsv` dumps every rotation+position in the heap (about 10 s).
2. Samples taken while idle, after turning the camera only, and after walking 10 m are compared
   with `tools/compare_transforms.py` to single out camera and player.
3. `fc6probe ptrscan <addr> 3 800` finds static chains; `fc6probe chain ...` validates them.

```
